================================================
FILE: do_tabstar.py
================================================
from importlib.resources import files
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split

from tabstar.tabstar_model import TabSTARClassifier


csv_path = files("tabstar").joinpath("resources", "imdb.csv")
x = pd.read_csv(csv_path)
y = x.pop('Genre_is_Drama')
x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(x, y, test_size=0.1)
tabstar = TabSTARClassifier(max_epochs=10)
tabstar.fit(x_train, y_train)
# tabstar.save("my_model_path.pkl")
# tabstar = TabSTARClassifier.load("my_model_path.pkl")
metric = tabstar.score(X=x_test, y=y_test)
print(f"AUC: {metric:.4f}")

================================================
FILE: README-pypi.md
================================================
<img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/alanarazi7/TabSTAR/main/figures/tabstar_logo.png" alt="TabSTAR Logo" width="50%">

ðŸ“š [TabSTAR: A Tabular Foundation Model for Tabular Data with Text Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18125)

---

## ðŸš€ TabSTAR v1.1 is out

We released **TabSTAR v1.1**, a new generation of pretrained TabSTAR models. It ships alongside the original models, in its own module, so existing code keeps working unchanged:

| | import | estimators |
|---|---|---|
| TabSTAR | `tabstar.tabstar_model` | `TabSTARClassifier`, `TabSTARRegressor` |
| TabSTAR v1.1 | `tabstar.tabstarv1p1_model` | `TabSTARv1p1Classifier`, `TabSTARv1p1Regressor` |

The v1.1 pretraining code lives in [`tabstarv1p1_pretrain/`](https://github.com/alanarazi7/TabSTAR/tree/master/tabstarv1p1_pretrain); a detailed writeup of what changed will follow.

---

## Install

To fit a pretrained TabSTAR model to your own dataset, install the package using:

```bash
pip install tabstar
```
---

## Quickstart Example

You can quickly get started with TabSTAR using the following example.

```python
from importlib.resources import files
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split

from tabstar.tabstar_model import TabSTARClassifier

csv_path = files("tabstar").joinpath("resources", "imdb.csv")
x = pd.read_csv(csv_path)
y = x.pop('Genre_is_Drama')
x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(x, y, test_size=0.1)
# For regression tasks, replace `TabSTARClassifier` with `TabSTARRegressor`.
tabstar = TabSTARClassifier()
tabstar.fit(x_train, y_train)
# tabstar.save("my_model_path.pkl")
# tabstar = TabSTARClassifier.load("my_model_path.pkl")
# y_pred = tabstar.predict(x_test)
metric = tabstar.score(X=x_test, y=y_test)
print(f"AUC: {metric:.4f}")
```

To use TabSTAR v1.1 instead, swap the import and the class name:

```python
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1_model import TabSTARv1p1Classifier, TabSTARv1p1Regressor

tabstar = TabSTARv1p1Classifier()
tabstar.fit(x_train, y_train)
y_pred = tabstar.predict(x_test)
metric = tabstar.score(X=x_test, y=y_test)
```

`fit`, `predict`, `predict_proba`, `score`, `save` and `load` behave as they do for the original models.

Model cards: [classification](https://github.com/alanarazi7/TabSTAR/blob/master/docs/model_cards/tabstar-v1.1-cls.md), [regression](https://github.com/alanarazi7/TabSTAR/blob/master/docs/model_cards/tabstar-v1.1-reg.md).

For paper replication, TabSTAR evaluation on benchmarks, custom pretraining or research purposes, see:

ðŸ”— [TabSTAR Research Repository](https://github.com/alanarazi7/TabSTAR)
---

@inproceedings{

---
library_name: tabstar
tags:
- tabular
- classification
---

# TabSTAR v1.1 classification

`alana89/TabSTAR-v1.1-cls-20261011`, the classification checkpoint behind
`tabstar.tabstarv1p1_model.TabSTARv1p1Classifier`.

```python
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1_model import TabSTARv1p1Classifier

model = TabSTARv1p1Classifier()
model.fit(x_train, y_train)
y_pred = model.predict(x_test)
```

The checkpoint is resolved automatically; `pretrain_dataset_or_path` overrides it.

## Pretraining

- **Task type**: classification only, a separate model per task type
- **Pool**: 200,142 classification tables, decontaminated against the evaluation suites
- **Dataset sampling**: 50,000 datasets per epoch, resampled from the full pool at the start of every epoch
- **Learning rate**: 1e-4, **weight decay**: 1e-3, linear warmup over the first 10% of steps
- **Epochs**: 25
- **Batch size**: 512 global (micro-batch 32, no gradient accumulation)
- **Text encoder**: `intfloat/e5-small-v2`, top 6 layers unfrozen, text batch size 1024
- **Tabular backbone**: 6 interaction layers, `d_model` 384
- **Numeric features**: each numeric cell becomes a 6-dimensional vector (clipped z-score, min-max,
  uniform quantile, outlier flag, missing flag, numeric indicator) through an MLP
- **Checkpoint selection**: downstream finetuning Elo, measured every epoch, rather than pretraining loss

## Downstream finetuning

The package defaults, which are also what the checkpoint was selected under, none of them tuned per
dataset:

- LoRA rank 32, alpha 2x rank, dropout 0.1, learning rate 1e-3, weight decay 0
- Batch size 128, up to 50 epochs, early-stopping patience 5
- Predictions averaged over the three best-validation-loss epochs

## Differences from the original TabSTAR (v1)

- **Pretraining data**: filtered by content-overlap sketches over column value sets and schemas,
  which drop duplicate tables and copies of evaluation tables
- **Scale**: 50,000 datasets per epoch, resampled each epoch from a pool of ~200K tables
- **Task separation**: classification and regression are pretrained as separate models, not one
  mixed model
- **Global batch 512**: larger and more diverse per step than v1
- **Selection metric**: downstream finetuning Elo rather than pretraining loss
- **Fusion**: each column's text and numeric embeddings are layer-normalised and summed, with a
  learned scalar weight on the text stream, rather than the transformer fusion block v1 used
- **Numeric representation**: six channels per numeric cell instead of v1's single scaled scalar,
  so outliers and missingness reach the model explicitly
- **Text encoder and width** are unchanged from v1 (E5-small-v2, `d_model` 384)

## Caveats

- Epoch-to-epoch selection scores are noisy, so the chosen epoch is partly selection luck.
- Selection scores are computed separately per task type; this checkpoint is only evaluated on
  classification.

---
library_name: tabstar

# TabSTAR v1.1 regression

`alana89/TabSTAR-v1.1-reg-20261009`, the regression checkpoint behind
`tabstar.tabstarv1p1_model.TabSTARv1p1Regressor`.

```python
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1_model import TabSTARv1p1Regressor

model = TabSTARv1p1Regressor()
model.fit(x_train, y_train)
y_pred = model.predict(x_test)
```

The checkpoint is resolved automatically; `pretrain_dataset_or_path` overrides it.

## Pretraining

- **Task type**: regression only, a separate model per task type
- **Pool**: 200,013 regression tables, decontaminated against the evaluation suites
- **Dataset sampling**: 50,000 datasets per epoch, resampled from the full pool at the start of every epoch
- **Learning rate**: 1e-4, **weight decay**: 1e-3, linear warmup over the first 10% of steps
- **Epochs**: 25
- **Batch size**: 512 global (micro-batch 32, no gradient accumulation)
- **Text encoder**: `intfloat/e5-small-v2`, top 6 layers unfrozen, text batch size 1024
- **Tabular backbone**: 6 interaction layers, `d_model` 384
- **Numeric features**: each numeric cell becomes a 6-dimensional vector (clipped z-score, min-max,
  uniform quantile, outlier flag, missing flag, numeric indicator) through an MLP
- **Regression head**: a scalar sub-head plus a distributional sub-head over 200 bins on z-scored
  targets clipped to +/-10
- **Loss**: 0.5 * MSE (scalar) + 0.5 * distributional loss, itself 0.5 * cross-entropy + 0.5 * CRPS
  over the 200 bin centers
- **Checkpoint selection**: downstream finetuning Elo, measured every epoch, rather than pretraining loss

## Downstream finetuning

The package defaults, which are also what the checkpoint was selected under, none of them tuned per
dataset:

- LoRA rank 32, alpha 2x rank, dropout 0.1, learning rate 1e-3, weight decay 0
- Batch size 128, up to 50 epochs, early-stopping patience 5
- Predictions averaged over the three best-validation-loss epochs

## Differences from the original TabSTAR (v1)

- **Pretraining data**: filtered by content-overlap sketches over column value sets and schemas,
  which drop duplicate tables and copies of evaluation tables
- **Scale**: 50,000 datasets per epoch, resampled each epoch from a pool of ~200K tables
- **Task separation**: classification and regression are pretrained as separate models, not one
  mixed model
- **Global batch 512**: larger and more diverse per step than v1
- **Selection metric**: downstream finetuning Elo rather than pretraining loss
- **Fusion**: each column's text and numeric embeddings are layer-normalised and summed, with a
  learned scalar weight on the text stream, rather than the transformer fusion block v1 used
- **Numeric representation**: six channels per numeric cell instead of v1's single scaled scalar,
  so outliers and missingness reach the model explicitly
- **Regression head and loss**: a dual scalar plus distributional head (200 bins, z-clip +/-10)
  trained with MSE plus a CE/CRPS blend, instead of a single scalar head
- **Text encoder and width** are unchanged from v1 (E5-small-v2, `d_model` 384)

## Caveats

- Epoch-to-epoch selection scores are noisy, so the chosen epoch is partly selection luck.
- Selection scores are computed separately per task type; this checkpoint is only evaluated on
  regression.

import numpy as np
import torch
from torch import Tensor
from transformers import AutoTokenizer, AutoModel, PreTrainedModel
from tabstar.arch.config import TabStarConfig, E5_SMALL
from tabstar.arch.interaction import InteractionEncoder
from tabstar.arch.fusion import NumericalFusion
from tabstar.arch.prediction import PredictionHead
from tabstar.constants import E5_SMALL_LOCAL_PATH
from tabstar.training.devices import clear_cuda_cache


class TabStarModel(PreTrainedModel):
    config_class = TabStarConfig

    def __init__(self, config: TabStarConfig):
        super().__init__(config)
        e5_path = E5_SMALL_LOCAL_PATH or E5_SMALL
        self.text_encoder = AutoModel.from_pretrained(e5_path)
        self.tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(e5_path)
        self.numerical_fusion = NumericalFusion()
        self.tabular_encoder = InteractionEncoder()
        self.cls_head = PredictionHead()
        self.reg_head = PredictionHead()
        self.post_init()

    def forward(self, x_txt: np.ndarray, x_num: np.ndarray, d_output: int) -> Tensor:
        textual_embeddings = self.get_textual_embedding(x_txt)
        if not isinstance(x_num, Tensor):
            # TODO: this is a bug, it should always be a Tensor
            x_num = torch.tensor(x_num, dtype=textual_embeddings.dtype, device=textual_embeddings.device)
        embeddings = self.numerical_fusion(textual_embeddings=textual_embeddings, x_num=x_num)
        encoded = self.tabular_encoder(embeddings)
        target_tokens = encoded[:, :d_output]
        if d_output == 1:
            target_scores = self.reg_head(target_tokens)
        else:
            target_scores = self.cls_head(target_tokens)
        target_scores = target_scores.squeeze(dim=-1)
        assert tuple(target_scores.shape) == (x_txt.shape[0], d_output)
        return target_scores

    def get_textual_embedding(self, x_txt: np.array) -> Tensor:
        text_batch_size = 128
        while text_batch_size > 1:
            try:
                return self.get_textual_embedding_in_batches(x_txt, text_batch_size=text_batch_size)
            except torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError:
                text_batch_size //= 2
                clear_cuda_cache()
                print(f"ðŸ¤¯ Reducing batch size to {text_batch_size} due to OOM")
        raise RuntimeError(f"ðŸ¤¯ OOM even with batch size 1!")

    def get_textual_embedding_in_batches(self, x_txt: np.array, text_batch_size: int) -> Tensor:
        # Get unique texts and mapping indices
        unique_texts, inverse_indices = np.unique(x_txt, return_inverse=True)
        num_unique_texts = len(unique_texts)
        embeddings = []
        for i in range(0, num_unique_texts, text_batch_size):
            batch_texts = unique_texts[i:i + text_batch_size].tolist()
            inputs = self.tokenizer(batch_texts, padding=True, return_tensors='pt', truncation=True)
            inputs = {k: v.to(self.device) for k, v in inputs.items()}
            outputs = self.text_encoder(**inputs)
            # Take the [CLS] token representation
            embeddings.append(outputs.last_hidden_state[:, 0, :])
        embeddings = torch.cat(embeddings, dim=0)
        inverse_indices = torch.tensor(inverse_indices, dtype=torch.long, device=embeddings.device)
        # Map the unique embeddings back to the original positions and reshape to match the original dimension
        batch_size, seq_len = x_txt.shape
        embeddings = embeddings[inverse_indices].view(batch_size, seq_len, -1)
        if not tuple(embeddings.shape) == (batch_size, seq_len, self.config.d_model):
            raise RuntimeError(f"Unexpected embedding shape: {embeddings.shape}")
        return embeddings

FILE: src/tabstar/arch/config.py

from transformers import PretrainedConfig


D_MODEL = 384
E5_SMALL = 'intfloat/e5-small-v2'
WEIGHT_DECAY = 0.001

# TODO: this actually should include many arch parameters: /tabular/tabstar/params/config.py
class TabStarConfig(PretrainedConfig):
    model_type = "tabstar"

    def __init__(
        self,
        d_model: int = D_MODEL,
        embedding_model: str = E5_SMALL,
        weight_decay: float = WEIGHT_DECAY,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        super().__init__(**kwargs)
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.embedding_model = embedding_model
        self.weight_decay = weight_decay



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/arch/fusion.py
================================================
import torch
from torch import nn, Tensor

from tabstar.arch.config import D_MODEL


class NumericalFusion(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.scalar_embedder = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(1, D_MODEL * 2),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Dropout(0.1),
            nn.Linear(D_MODEL * 2, D_MODEL)
        )
        self.fusion_block = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
            d_model=D_MODEL,
            nhead=2,
            dim_feedforward=D_MODEL * 4,
            dropout=0.1,
            activation='relu',
            batch_first=True,
            norm_first=True
        )


    def forward(self, textual_embeddings: Tensor, x_num: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        batch_size, seq_len, d_model = textual_embeddings.shape
        num_embeddings = self.scalar_embedder(x_num.unsqueeze(-1))
        assert num_embeddings.shape == textual_embeddings.shape
        fusion_input = torch.stack([textual_embeddings, num_embeddings], dim=2)
        assert fusion_input.shape == (batch_size, seq_len, 2, d_model)
        fusion_input = fusion_input.view(batch_size * seq_len, 2, d_model)
        fused = self.fusion_block(fusion_input)
        fused_embeddings = fused.view(batch_size, seq_len, 2, d_model)
        fused_embeddings = fused_embeddings.mean(dim=2)
        assert fused_embeddings.shape == textual_embeddings.shape
        return fused_embeddings



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/arch/interaction.py
================================================
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

from tabstar.arch.config import D_MODEL


class InteractionEncoder(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, num_layers: int = 6, d_model: int = D_MODEL, num_heads_factor: int = 64,
                 ffn_d_hidden_multiplier: int = 4, dropout: float = 0.1):
        super().__init__()
        dim_feedforward = d_model * ffn_d_hidden_multiplier
        num_heads = d_model // num_heads_factor
        encoder_layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
                d_model=d_model,
                nhead=num_heads,
                dim_feedforward=dim_feedforward,
                dropout=dropout,
                activation='relu',
                batch_first=True,
                norm_first=True
            )
        self.encoder = nn.TransformerEncoder(encoder_layer, num_layers=num_layers, enable_nested_tensor=False)

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.encoder(x)



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/arch/prediction.py
================================================
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

from tabstar.arch.config import D_MODEL


class PredictionHead(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, input_size: int = D_MODEL):
        super().__init__()
        hidden_size = input_size * 4
        self.layers = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(input_size, hidden_size),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden_size, 1)
        )

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.layers(x)

class UrlDatasetID(Enum):
    REG_CONSUMER_BABIES_R_US_PRICES = "http://pages.cs.wisc.edu/~anhai/data/784_data/baby_products/csv_files/babies_r_us.csv"
    REG_CONSUMER_BIKE_PRICE_BIKEWALE = "http://pages.cs.wisc.edu/~anhai/data/784_data/bikes/csv_files/bikewale.csv"
    REG_PROFESSIONAL_EMPLOYEE_RENUMERATION_VANCOUBER = "https://opendata.vancouver.ca/api/records/1.0/download/?dataset=employee-remuneration-and-expenses-earning-over-75000&format=csv"
    REG_PROFESSIONAL_ML_DS_AI_JOBS_SALARIES = "https://ai-jobs.net/salaries/download/salaries.csv"
    REG_PROFESSIONAL_SCIMAGOJR_ACADEMIC_IMPACT = "https://www.scimagojr.com/journalrank.php?out=xls"
    REG_SOCIAL_BOOKS_GOODREADS = "http://pages.cs.wisc.edu/~anhai/data/784_data/books2/csv_files/goodreads.csv"
    REG_SOCIAL_MOVIES_ROTTEN_TOMATOES = "http://pages.cs.wisc.edu/~anhai/data/784_data/movies1/csv_files/rotten_tomatoes.csv"


TabularDatasetID = OpenMLDatasetID | KaggleDatasetID | UrlDatasetID

FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/binning.py
================================================
from typing import List

import numpy as np
from pandas import Series
from sklearn.preprocessing import QuantileTransformer

from tabstar.preprocessing.nulls import get_invalid_indices, MISSING_VALUE
from tabstar.preprocessing.verbalize import verbalize_feature

VERBALIZED_QUANTILE_BINS = 10

def fit_numerical_bins(s: Series) -> QuantileTransformer:
    s = s.copy().dropna()
    scaler = QuantileTransformer(output_distribution='uniform',
                                 n_quantiles=min(1000, len(s)),
                                 subsample=1000000000,
                                 random_state=0)
    scaler.fit(s.values.reshape(-1, 1))
    return scaler


def transform_numerical_bins(s: Series, scaler: QuantileTransformer) -> Series:
    invalid_indices = get_invalid_indices(s)
    quantile_levels = np.linspace(0, 1, VERBALIZED_QUANTILE_BINS + 1)
    boundaries = scaler.inverse_transform(quantile_levels.reshape(-1, 1)).flatten()
    assert len(boundaries) == VERBALIZED_QUANTILE_BINS + 1
    verbalized_bins = verbalize_bins(boundaries)
    bin_index = np.digitize(s, boundaries)
    verbalized = [verbalized_bins[i] for i in bin_index]
    for idx in invalid_indices:
        verbalized[idx] = MISSING_VALUE
    verbalized = [verbalize_feature(col=str(s.name), value=v) for v in verbalized]
    s = Series(verbalized, index=s.index, name=s.name)
    return s


def verbalize_bins(boundaries: np.array) -> List[str]:
    # TODO: this can become a bit ugly with high-precision numbers, or relatively-discrete numerical values
    boundaries = [format_float(b) for b in boundaries]
    first = f"Lower than {min(boundaries)} (Quantile 0%)"
    last = f"Higher than {max(boundaries)} (Quantile 100%)"
    bins = []
    for i, b in enumerate(boundaries[:-1]):
        r = f"{b} to {boundaries[i + 1]}"
        low = i * VERBALIZED_QUANTILE_BINS
        high = (i + 1) * VERBALIZED_QUANTILE_BINS
        q = f"(Quantile {low} - {high}%)"
        bins.append(f"{r} {q}")
    assert len(bins) == VERBALIZED_QUANTILE_BINS == len(boundaries) - 1
    bins = [first] + bins + [last]
    return bins


def format_float(num: float) -> str:
    rounded = round(num, 4)
    if rounded.is_integer():
        return str(int(rounded))
    formatted = f"{rounded:.4f}"
    formatted = formatted.rstrip("0").rstrip(".")
    return formatted

FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/dates.py
================================================
from typing import Any, Dict

import pandas as pd
from pandas import Series, DataFrame
from pandas.core.dtypes.common import is_datetime64_any_dtype
from skrub import DatetimeEncoder


def transform_date_features(x: DataFrame, date_transformers: Dict[str, DatetimeEncoder]) -> DataFrame:
    rows = x.shape[0]
    for col, dt_encoder in date_transformers.items():
        s = series_to_dt(s=x[col])
        dt_df = dt_encoder.transform(s)
        dt_df.index = x.index
        x = x.drop(columns=[col])
        x = pd.concat([x, dt_df], axis=1)
        if x.shape[0] != rows:
            raise ValueError(f"Row mismatch after transforming date column {col}: expected {rows}, got {x.shape}")
    return x

def fit_date_encoders(x: DataFrame) -> Dict[str, DatetimeEncoder]:
    date_encoders = {}
    date_columns = [str(col) for col, dtype in x.dtypes.items() if is_datetime64_any_dtype(dtype)]
    for col in date_columns:
        dt_s = series_to_dt(s=x[col])
        # Adds: "year", "month", "day", "hour", "total_seconds", "weekday"
        encoder = DatetimeEncoder(add_weekday=True, add_total_seconds=True)
        encoder.fit(dt_s)
        date_encoders[col] = encoder
    return date_encoders

def series_to_dt(s: Series) -> Series:
    # TODO: do we want to handle missing values here?
    s = s.apply(_clean_dirty_date)
    dt_s = pd.to_datetime(s, errors='coerce')
    dt_s = dt_s.apply(_remove_timezone)
    return dt_s


def _remove_timezone(dt):
    if pd.notnull(dt) and getattr(dt, 'tzinfo', None) is not None:
        return dt.tz_localize(None)
    return dt


def _clean_dirty_date(s: Any) -> Any:
    if isinstance(s, str):
        s = s.replace('"', "")
    return s

FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/detection.py
================================================
from typing import Any

from pandas import Series

from tabstar.preprocessing.nulls import get_valid_values

MAX_NUMERIC_FOR_CATEGORICAL = 50


def is_mostly_numerical(s: Series) -> bool:
    values = get_valid_values(s)
    unique = set(values)
    n_unique = len(unique)
    if n_unique <= MAX_NUMERIC_FOR_CATEGORICAL:
        return False
    non_numerical_unique = [v for v in unique if not is_numeric(v)]
    if len(non_numerical_unique) > 1:
        return False
    return True



def is_numeric(f: Any) -> bool:
    if f is None:
        return False
    if isinstance(f, str):
        return f.isdigit()
    if isinstance(f, (int, float,)):
        return True
    try:
        f = float(f)
        return True
    except ValueError:
        print(f"ValueError: {f} from type {f} cannot be converted to float")
        return False


================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/feat_types.py
================================================
from typing import Set, Optional

import pandas as pd
from pandas import DataFrame, Series
from pandas.core.dtypes.common import is_datetime64_any_dtype, is_numeric_dtype, is_object_dtype, is_bool_dtype

from tabstar.preprocessing.detection import is_mostly_numerical, is_numeric
from tabstar.preprocessing.nulls import convert_numeric_with_missing, MISSING_VALUE, get_valid_values

pd.set_option('future.no_silent_downcasting', True)


def transform_feature_types(x: DataFrame, numerical_features: Set[str]) -> DataFrame:
    new_x = {}
    for col in x.columns:
        if col in numerical_features:
            new_x[col] = convert_series_to_numeric(s=x[col])
        else:
            new_x[col] = convert_series_to_textual(s=x[col])
    new_x = pd.DataFrame(new_x, index=x.index)
    ordered_x = new_x[x.columns]
    return ordered_x


def detect_numerical_features(x: DataFrame) -> Set[str]:
    return {col for col in x.columns if is_numerical_feature(s=x[col])}


# TODO: for future versions, maybe best to rely on maintained packages, e.g. skrub's TableVectorizer
def is_numerical_feature(s: Series) -> bool:
    if is_datetime64_any_dtype(s.dtype):
        raise TypeError(f"At this point, dates should have already been transformed.")
    elif len(get_valid_values(s)) == 0:
        return False
    elif is_numeric_dtype(s.dtype) or is_mostly_numerical(s=s):
        return True
    elif is_object_dtype(s.dtype) or is_bool_dtype(s.dtype) or isinstance(s.dtype, pd.CategoricalDtype):
        return False
    else:
        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported dtype {s.dtype} for series {s.name}")


def convert_series_to_numeric(s: Series, missing_value: Optional[str] = None) -> Series:
    if pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(s):
        return s.astype(float)
    non_numeric_indices = [not is_numeric(f) for f in s]
    if not any(non_numeric_indices):
        return s.astype(float)
    if missing_value is None:
        unique_non_numeric = s[non_numeric_indices].unique()
        if len(unique_non_numeric) != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"Missing values detected are {unique_non_numeric}. Should be only one!")
        missing_value = unique_non_numeric[0]
    return convert_numeric_with_missing(s=s, missing_value=missing_value)


def convert_series_to_textual(s: Series):
    return s.astype(object).fillna(MISSING_VALUE).astype(str)


================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/nulls.py
================================================
from typing import List, Any, Optional, Set

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas import Series

MISSING_VALUE = "Unknown Value"


def raise_if_null_target(y: Series):
    # TODO: do we want to allow throwing these away with a flag?
    # TODO: for multiclass, warn if any category is too rare?
    missing = y.isnull()
    y_missing = missing.sum()
    if y_missing > 0:
        raise ValueError(f"Target variable {y.name} has {y_missing} null values, please handle them before training.")


def get_invalid_indices(ls: Series) -> Set[int]:
    return {i for i, x in enumerate(ls) if _get_non_null_value(x) is None}


def get_valid_values(ls: Series) -> List:
    return [x for x in ls if _get_non_null_value(x) is not None]

def _get_non_null_value(x: Any) -> Optional[Any]:
    if isinstance(x, str):
        return x
    if isinstance(x, (list, np.ndarray)):
        return x if len(x) > 0 else None
    if pd.isna(x):
        return None
    if not np.isfinite(x):
        return None
    if pd.isnull(x):
        return None
    return x


def convert_numeric_with_missing(s: pd.Series, missing_value: Any) -> pd.Series:
    return s.apply(lambda x: x if x != missing_value else np.nan).astype(float)



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/scaler.py
================================================
from pandas import Series
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler

from tabstar.preprocessing.nulls import get_invalid_indices

Z_MAX_ABS_VAL = 3


def fit_standard_scaler(s: Series) -> StandardScaler:
    s = s.copy().dropna()
    scaler = StandardScaler()
    scaler.fit(s.values.reshape(-1, 1))
    return scaler

def transform_clipped_z_scores(s: Series, scaler: StandardScaler, allow_null: bool = False) -> Series:
    invalid = get_invalid_indices(s)
    s = s.copy()
    if allow_null:
        s = s.fillna(s.mean())
    s_val = scaler.transform(s.values.reshape(-1, 1)).flatten()
    s_val = s_val.clip(-Z_MAX_ABS_VAL, Z_MAX_ABS_VAL)
    if invalid:
        s_val[sorted(invalid)] = 0
    s = Series(s_val, index=s.index, name=s.name)
    return s

from typing import Tuple, Optional

from pandas import DataFrame, Series, SparseDtype


def densify_objects(x: DataFrame, y: Optional[Series]) -> Tuple[DataFrame, Optional[Series]]:
    for col in x.columns:
        x[col] = densify_series(x[col])
    if y is not None:
        y = densify_series(y)
    return x, y



def densify_series(s: Series) -> Series:
    if not isinstance(s.dtype, SparseDtype):
        return s
    return s.sparse.to_dense()


================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/splits.py
================================================
import random
from typing import Tuple, Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas import DataFrame, Series
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split

from tabstar.constants import SEED

TEST_RATIO = 0.1
MAX_TEST_SIZE = 2000
VAL_RATIO = 0.1
MAX_VAL_SIZE = 1000

def split_to_test(x: DataFrame, y: Series, is_cls: bool, fold: int = -1, train_examples: Optional[int] = None) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    test_size = int(len(y) * TEST_RATIO)
    test_size = min(test_size, MAX_TEST_SIZE)
    if (train_examples is not None) and len(x) > train_examples:
        test_size = len(x) - train_examples
    x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = do_split(x=x, y=y, test_size=test_size, is_cls=is_cls, fold=fold)
    return x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test

def split_to_val(x: DataFrame, y: Series, is_cls: bool, fold: int = -1, val_ratio: float = VAL_RATIO) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    val_size = int(len(y) * val_ratio)
    val_size = min(val_size, MAX_VAL_SIZE)
    x_train, x_val, y_train, y_val = do_split(x=x, y=y, test_size=val_size, is_cls=is_cls, fold=fold)
    return x_train, x_val, y_train, y_val


def do_split(x: DataFrame, y: Series, test_size: int, is_cls: bool, fold: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    if not x.index.equals(y.index):
        raise ValueError("X and y must share identical indices. Construct y as: y = pd.Series(y, index=X.index)")
    random_state = SEED + fold
    if not is_cls:
        return train_test_split(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state)
    num_classes = y.nunique()
    if num_classes == 2:
        return _split_for_binary_classification(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state)
    has_rare_class = y.value_counts().min() <= 1
    if has_rare_class:
        return _split_with_rare_classes(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state)
    return train_test_split(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state, stratify=y)


def _split_with_rare_classes(x: DataFrame, y: Series, test_size: int, random_state: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    # TODO: add tests here, seems like a complex function
    singleton_classes = y.value_counts()[y.value_counts() == 1].index
    is_singleton = y.isin(singleton_classes)
    x_single = x[is_singleton]
    y_single = y[is_singleton]
    x_rest = x[~is_singleton]
    y_rest = y[~is_singleton]

    rest_classes = len(set(y_rest))
    test_size = max(test_size, rest_classes)
    x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(x_rest, y_rest, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state,
                                                        stratify=y_rest)
    x_train, y_train = merge_splits(x1=x_train, x2=x_single, y1=y_train, y2=y_single, random_state=random_state)
    return x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test


def _split_for_binary_classification(x: DataFrame, y: Series, test_size: int, random_state: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    test_indices = []
    y_values = sorted(set(y))
    assert len(y_values) == 2, f"Expected 2 classes, got {y_values}"
    random_number_generator = np.random.RandomState(random_state)
    for v in y_values:
        v_indices = y[y == v].index.tolist()
        v_idx = random_number_generator.choice(v_indices)
        test_indices.append(v_idx)
    x_single_test = x.loc[test_indices]
    y_single_test = y.loc[test_indices]
    x_rest = x.drop(index=test_indices)
    y_rest = y.drop(index=test_indices)
    x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(x_rest, y_rest, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state,
                                                        stratify=y_rest)
    x_test, y_test = merge_splits(x1=x_test, x2=x_single_test, y1=y_test, y2=y_single_test, random_state=random_state)
    return x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test



def merge_splits(x1: DataFrame, x2: DataFrame, y1: Series, y2: Series, random_state: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, Series]:
    x_train = pd.concat([x1, x2])
    y_train = pd.concat([y1, y2])
    shuffled = x_train.sample(frac=1, random_state=random_state).index
    x = x_train.loc[shuffled]
    y = y_train.loc[shuffled]
    return x, y

FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/target.py
================================================
import numpy as np
from pandas import Series
from sklearn.preprocessing import LabelEncoder, StandardScaler

from tabstar.preprocessing.scaler import fit_standard_scaler, transform_clipped_z_scores


def transform_preprocess_y(y: Series | np.ndarray, scaler: LabelEncoder | StandardScaler) -> Series:
    y = y.copy()
    if isinstance(scaler, StandardScaler):
        return transform_clipped_z_scores(s=y, scaler=scaler)
    elif isinstance(scaler, LabelEncoder):
        return transform_cls_y(y=y, encoder=scaler)
    raise TypeError(f"What is this scaler {scaler} from type {type(scaler)}?")


def transform_cls_y(y: Series, encoder: LabelEncoder) -> Series:
    y_val = encoder.transform(y)
    return Series(y_val, name=y.name, index=y.index)


def fit_preprocess_y(y: Series, is_cls: bool) -> LabelEncoder | StandardScaler:
    if is_cls:
        return fit_cls_y(y)
    else:
        return fit_reg_y(y)

def fit_cls_y(y: Series) -> LabelEncoder:
    label_encoder = LabelEncoder()
    label_encoder.fit(y)
    return label_encoder

def fit_reg_y(y: Series) -> StandardScaler:
    return fit_standard_scaler(s=y)

FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/texts.py

import re
from typing import Tuple, Optional

from pandas import DataFrame, Series


def replace_column_names(x: DataFrame, y: Optional[Series]) -> Tuple[DataFrame, Optional[Series]]:
    old2new = {c: normalize_col_name(c) for c in x.columns}
    x.rename(columns=old2new, inplace=True)
    if y is not None:
        y.name = normalize_col_name(str(y.name))
    return x, y

def normalize_col_name(text: str) -> str:
    for c in ['\n', '\r', '\t', '\u00A0']:
        text = text.replace(c, ' ')
    text = replace_unspaced_symbols(text)
    text = replace_whitespaces(text)
    return text

def replace_unspaced_symbols(text: str) -> str:
    if ' ' not in text:
        return text
    for c in ['_', '-', ".", ":"]:
        text = text.replace(c, ' ')
    return text

def replace_whitespaces(text: str) -> str:
    # TODO: originally this was supposed to keep newlines and tabs
    return re.sub(r'[\x00-\x1F\x7F]', ' ', text)



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/preprocessing/verbalize.py
================================================
from typing import List, Optional

import pandas as pd
from pandas import DataFrame
from pandas.core.dtypes.common import is_numeric_dtype

from tabstar.preprocessing.texts import replace_whitespaces


def verbalize_textual_features(x: DataFrame) -> DataFrame:
    semantic_cols = [col for col, dtype in x.dtypes.items() if not is_numeric_dtype(dtype)]
    for col in semantic_cols:
        x[col] = x[col].apply(lambda v: verbalize_feature(col=str(col), value=v))
    return x

def verbalize_feature(col: str, value: str) -> str:
    if not isinstance(value, str):
        raise TypeError(f"Expected string value, got {type(value)}")
    col = replace_whitespaces(col)
    value = replace_whitespaces(value)
    v = f"Predictive Feature: {col}\nFeature Value: {value}"
    return v

def prepend_target_tokens(x: DataFrame, y_name: str, y_values: Optional[List[str]]) -> DataFrame:
    y_name = replace_whitespaces(y_name)
    if y_values:
        values = [replace_whitespaces(text=str(v)) for v in y_values]
        tokens = [f"Target Feature: {y_name}\nFeature Value: {v}" for v in values]
    else:
        tokens = [f"Numerical Target Feature: {y_name}"]
    target_df = DataFrame({f"TABSTAR_TARGET_TOKEN_{i}": [t] * len(x) for i, t in enumerate(tokens)}, index=x.index)
    x = pd.concat([target_df, x], axis=1)
    return x

================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/tabstar_checkpoints.py
================================================
from typing import Optional

from huggingface_hub import snapshot_download
from huggingface_hub.errors import LocalEntryNotFoundError
from transformers import AutoTokenizer

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import TEXT_MODEL

DEFAULT_CLS_CHECKPOINT = "alana89/TabSTAR-v1.1-cls-20261011"
DEFAULT_REG_CHECKPOINT = "alana89/TabSTAR-v1.1-reg-20261009"


def get_tabstar_version(pretrain_dataset_or_path: Optional[str] = None, is_cls: Optional[bool] = None) -> str:
    if pretrain_dataset_or_path is not None:
        if isinstance(pretrain_dataset_or_path, str):
            return pretrain_dataset_or_path
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown pretrain_dataset_or_path: {pretrain_dataset_or_path}")
    if is_cls is None:
        raise ValueError("is_cls is required to resolve a default checkpoint")
    return DEFAULT_CLS_CHECKPOINT if is_cls else DEFAULT_REG_CHECKPOINT


def download_tabstar(repo_id: Optional[str] = None):
    """Warm the cache before a fit, so an unreachable checkpoint fails here and not mid-training.
    Without a repo_id, both default checkpoints are fetched."""
    repo_ids = [repo_id] if repo_id is not None else [DEFAULT_CLS_CHECKPOINT, DEFAULT_REG_CHECKPOINT]
    for repo in repo_ids:
        _snapshot(repo)
    # The weights carry no tokenizer of their own; the text encoder's is fetched separately.
    AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(TEXT_MODEL)


def _snapshot(repo_id: str):
    try:
        snapshot_download(repo_id=repo_id, local_files_only=True)
    except LocalEntryNotFoundError:
        snapshot_download(repo_id=repo_id)

from transformers import PretrainedConfig

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import TEXT_BATCH_SIZE
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import D_MODEL, INTERACTION_LAYERS, TEXT_MODEL, D_NUM, REG_DIST_BINS
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import TEXTUAL_UNFREEZE_LAYERS


class TabStarConfig(PretrainedConfig):
    model_type = "tabstar"

    def __init__(
        self,
        interaction_layers: int = INTERACTION_LAYERS,
        d_model: int = D_MODEL,
        text_model: str = TEXT_MODEL,
        unfreeze_layers: int = TEXTUAL_UNFREEZE_LAYERS,
        text_batch_size: int = TEXT_BATCH_SIZE,
        d_num: int = D_NUM,
        reg_dist_bins: int = REG_DIST_BINS,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        super().__init__(**kwargs)
        self.interaction_layers = interaction_layers
        self.d_model = d_model
        self.text_model = text_model
        self.unfreeze_layers = unfreeze_layers
        self.text_batch_size = text_batch_size
        self.d_num = d_num
        self.reg_dist_bins = reg_dist_bins



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/arch/interaction.py
================================================
from typing import Optional

import torch.nn as nn
from torch import Tensor

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import D_MODEL


class InteractionEncoder(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, num_layers: int = 6, d_model: int = D_MODEL, num_heads_factor: int = 64,
                 ffn_d_hidden_multiplier: int = 4, dropout: float = 0.1):
        super().__init__()
        dim_feedforward = d_model * ffn_d_hidden_multiplier
        num_heads = d_model // num_heads_factor
        encoder_layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
                d_model=d_model,
                nhead=num_heads,
                dim_feedforward=dim_feedforward,
                dropout=dropout,
                activation='relu',
                batch_first=True,
                norm_first=True
            )
        self.encoder = nn.TransformerEncoder(encoder_layer, num_layers=num_layers, enable_nested_tensor=False)

    def forward(self, x: Tensor, feat_mask: Optional[Tensor] = None) -> Tensor:
        return self.encoder(x, src_key_padding_mask=feat_mask)

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/arch/model.py
================================================
import torch
from torch import nn, Tensor
from transformers import AutoModel, BatchEncoding, PreTrainedModel

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.training.devices import clear_cuda_cache

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.interaction import InteractionEncoder
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.naive_fusion import NaiveFusion
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.prediction import DualRegressionHead, PredictionHead
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.config import TabStarConfig
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.preprocessing.tokenization import trim_batch_to_max_size
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.numerical_encoder import NumericalEncoder
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.training.loss import mask_predictions
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.training.batching import TabSTARBatch
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.training.devices import get_precision


class TabSTARModel(PreTrainedModel):
    config_class = TabStarConfig

    def __init__(self, config: TabStarConfig):
        super().__init__(config)
        self.d_model = config.d_model
        self.d_num = config.d_num
        self.text_batch_size = config.text_batch_size
        self.text_encoder = AutoModel.from_pretrained(self.config.text_model)
        self.numerical_encoder = NumericalEncoder(d_model=self.d_model, d_num=self.d_num)
        self.fusion = NaiveFusion(d_model=self.d_model)
        self.tabular_encoder = InteractionEncoder(num_layers=config.interaction_layers, d_model=self.d_model)
        self.cls_head = PredictionHead(d_model=self.d_model)
        self.reg_dist_bins = config.reg_dist_bins
        self.reg_head = DualRegressionHead(d_model=self.d_model, reg_dist_bins=config.reg_dist_bins)
        self.post_init()

    def forward(self, batch: TabSTARBatch) -> Tensor:
        text_embeddings = self.get_textual_embeddings(x_txt=batch.x_txt, tokenized=batch.tokenized)
        numerical_embeddings = self.numerical_encoder(x_num=batch.x_num)
        embeddings = self.fusion(textual_embeddings=text_embeddings, num_embeddings=numerical_embeddings)
        encoded = self.tabular_encoder(embeddings, feat_mask=batch.feature_mask)
        targets = self.get_predictions(batch=batch, encoded=encoded)
        targets = mask_predictions(targets, d_outputs=batch.d_outputs)
        return targets

    def get_textual_embeddings(self, x_txt: Tensor, tokenized: BatchEncoding) -> Tensor:
        # Local to this call, not persisted on self: an OOM here must not permanently shrink
        # every later call on this model (e.g. a reused base model scoring many datasets).
        text_batch_size = self.text_batch_size
        while True:
            try:
                return self.get_textual_embeddings_in_batches(x_txt, tokenized=tokenized, text_batch_size=text_batch_size)
            except torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError:
                # Still an OOM, so the trainer can split the batch's rows, whose text activations it keeps for backward.
                if text_batch_size == 1:
                    raise
            # Retried outside the except block, so the failed attempt's tensors can be freed.
            text_batch_size //= 2
            clear_cuda_cache()
            print(f"ðŸ¤¯ Reducing batch size to {text_batch_size} due to OOM")

    def get_textual_embeddings_in_batches(self, x_txt: Tensor, tokenized: BatchEncoding, text_batch_size: int) -> Tensor:
        n_examples, elements = x_txt.shape
        num_unique_texts = tokenized["input_ids"].size(0)
        embeddings = []
        for i in range(0, num_unique_texts, text_batch_size):
            j = min(i + text_batch_size, num_unique_texts)
            batch_input = {k: v[i:j] for k, v in tokenized.items()}
            batch_input = trim_batch_to_max_size(batch_input)
            batch_input = {k: v.to(self.device, non_blocking=True) for k, v in batch_input.items()}
            batch_embeddings = self.text_encoder(**batch_input)
            batch_embeddings = batch_embeddings.last_hidden_state[:, 0, :].to(dtype=get_precision(device=self.device))
            embeddings.append(batch_embeddings)
        embeddings = torch.cat(embeddings, dim=0)
        embeddings = embeddings[x_txt]
        assert embeddings.shape == (n_examples, elements, self.d_model)
        return embeddings

    def get_predictions(self, batch: TabSTARBatch, encoded: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        batch_size, elements, d_num = batch.x_num.shape
        max_d_output = max(batch.d_outputs)
        targets = encoded[:, :max_d_output]
        head = self.cls_head if batch.is_cls else self.reg_head
        targets = head(targets)
        if batch.is_cls:
            targets = targets.squeeze(dim=-1)
            expected_shape = (batch_size, max_d_output)
        else:
            expected_shape = (batch_size, max_d_output, 1 + self.reg_dist_bins)
        if targets.shape != expected_shape:
            raise ValueError(f"Expected output shape {expected_shape}, got {targets.shape}")
        return targets

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/arch/naive_fusion.py
================================================
import torch
from torch import nn, Tensor

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import D_MODEL


class NaiveFusion(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model: int = D_MODEL):
        super().__init__()
        self.ln_text = nn.LayerNorm(d_model)
        self.ln_num = nn.LayerNorm(d_model)
        self.text_weight = nn.Parameter(torch.tensor(1.0))

    def forward(self, textual_embeddings: Tensor, num_embeddings: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        text_norm = self.ln_text(textual_embeddings) * self.text_weight.to(dtype=textual_embeddings.dtype)
        num_norm = self.ln_num(num_embeddings)
        fused_embeddings = text_norm + num_norm
        assert fused_embeddings.shape == textual_embeddings.shape == num_embeddings.shape
        return fused_embeddings

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/arch/numerical_encoder.py
================================================
from torch import nn, Tensor

class NumericalEncoder(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model: int, d_num: int):
        super().__init__()
        self.scalar_embedder = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(d_num, d_model * 2),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Dropout(0.1),
            nn.Linear(d_model * 2, d_model),
        )

    def forward(self, x_num: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        return self.scalar_embedder(x_num)



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/arch/prediction.py
================================================
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import D_MODEL, REG_Z_CLIP, REG_DIST_BINS


class PredictionHead(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, d_model: int = D_MODEL):
        super().__init__()
        hidden_size = d_model * 4
        self.layers = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(d_model, hidden_size),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden_size, 1)
        )

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.layers(x)


class DualRegressionHead(nn.Module):
    """A scalar sub-head and a distributional sub-head trained jointly; forward concatenates
    their outputs as (..., 1 + n_bins), scalar first, so a downstream consumer can blend both
    predictions at inference instead of relying on either alone."""

    def __init__(self, d_model: int = D_MODEL, reg_dist_bins: int = REG_DIST_BINS):
        super().__init__()
        self.scalar_head = PredictionHead(d_model=d_model)
        self.dist_head = DistributionalRegressionHead(d_model=d_model, reg_dist_bins=reg_dist_bins)

    @property
    def bin_edges(self) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.dist_head.bin_edges

    @property
    def bin_centers(self) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.dist_head.bin_centers

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        scalar_out = self.scalar_head(x)
        dist_out = self.dist_head(x)
        return torch.cat([scalar_out, dist_out], dim=-1)


class DistributionalRegressionHead(nn.Module):
    """Predicts a distribution over fixed z-score bins instead of a single scalar."""

    def __init__(self, d_model: int = D_MODEL, reg_dist_bins: int = REG_DIST_BINS):
        super().__init__()
        hidden_size = d_model * 4
        self.layers = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(d_model, hidden_size),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden_size, reg_dist_bins)
        )
        bin_edges = torch.linspace(-REG_Z_CLIP, REG_Z_CLIP, reg_dist_bins + 1)
        bin_centers = (bin_edges[:-1] + bin_edges[1:]) / 2
        self.register_buffer("bin_edges", bin_edges, persistent=False)
        self.register_buffer("bin_centers", bin_centers, persistent=False)

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.layers(x)

from datetime import datetime
from os import getpid, makedirs
from os.path import abspath, join
from typing import Optional

DOWNSTREAM_DIR = ".tabstar_downstream"


def make_run_dir(output_dir: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
    """Scratch directory for one finetune's saved weights, disambiguated so concurrent runs in the
    same working directory never read or delete each other's files."""
    run_id = f"{datetime.now().strftime('%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')}_{getpid()}"
    run_dir = abspath(join(output_dir or DOWNSTREAM_DIR, run_id))
    makedirs(run_dir, exist_ok=True)
    return run_dir



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/finetune/collator.py
================================================
from typing import List, Tuple

import numpy as np
import torch
from torch import Tensor
from torch.nn.utils.rnn import pad_sequence

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.preprocessing.tokenization import tokenize_x_txt
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import D_MODEL
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.training.batching import TabSTARBatch


def pretrain_batch_collator(examples) -> TabSTARBatch:
    x_num, x_txt, y, properties = zip(*examples)
    x_num, feature_mask = consolidate_x_num(x_num)
    x_txt, tokenized = tokenize_x_txt(x_txt=x_txt)
    assert all(isinstance(p, int) for p in properties) and len(set(properties)) == 1
    d_outputs = torch.tensor(properties, dtype=torch.long)
    y, is_cls = consolidate_y(y, d_outputs=d_outputs)
    batch = TabSTARBatch(x_num=x_num, x_txt=x_txt, tokenized=tokenized, y=y, d_outputs=d_outputs,
                          feature_mask=feature_mask, is_cls=is_cls)
    return batch

def consolidate_x_num(x_num: List[np.ndarray]) -> Tuple[Tensor, Tensor]:
    seq_tensors = [torch.as_tensor(x, dtype=torch.bfloat16) for x in x_num]
    x_num = pad_sequence(seq_tensors, batch_first=True, padding_value=float("nan"))
    feature_mask = torch.isnan(x_num)
    assert feature_mask.dim() == 3
    feature_mask = feature_mask.any(dim=-1)
    assert feature_mask.dim() == 2
    x_num = torch.nan_to_num(x_num, nan=0.0)
    return x_num, feature_mask

def consolidate_y(y: List[np.ndarray | int], d_outputs: Tensor) -> Tuple[Tensor, bool]:
    if max(d_outputs) == 1:
        dtype = torch.bfloat16
        is_cls = False
    elif min(d_outputs) >= 2:
        dtype = torch.long
        is_cls = True
    else:
        raise NotImplementedError("We don't support mixed classification and regression batches!")
    y = torch.tensor(y)
    y = y.to(dtype=dtype)
    return y, is_cls


def consolidate_x_txt(x_txt: List[np.ndarray]) -> Tensor:
    assert all(arr.shape[1] == D_MODEL and arr.ndim == 2 for arr in x_txt)
    max_cols = max(arr.shape[0] for arr in x_txt)
    batch_size = len(x_txt)
    x_txt = [torch.tensor(arr, dtype=torch.bfloat16) for arr in x_txt]
    x_txt = pad_sequence(x_txt, batch_first=True, padding_value=0.0)
    assert x_txt.shape == (batch_size, max_cols, D_MODEL)
    return x_txt

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/finetune/dataloader.py
================================================
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader

from torch.utils.data import Dataset

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.preprocessing.verbalizer import TabSTARData
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.training.hyperparams import GLOBAL_BATCH
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.finetune.collator import pretrain_batch_collator


class DownstreamDataset(Dataset):
    def __init__(self, data: TabSTARData):
        self.x_txt = data.x_txt
        self.x_num = data.x_num
        if isinstance(data.y, pd.Series):
            self.y = data.y.reset_index(drop=True)
        elif isinstance(data.y, np.ndarray):
            self.y = pd.Series(data.y, dtype=np.float32)
        else:
            # Dummy target for convenience
            self.y = pd.Series(np.zeros(len(data.x_txt)), dtype=np.float32)
        self.d_output = data.d_output

    def __len__(self):
        return len(self.y)

    def __getitem__(self, idx: int):
        x_txt = self.x_txt[idx]
        x_num = self.x_num[idx]
        y = self.y.iloc[idx]
        return x_num, x_txt, y, self.d_output
    
def get_dataloader(data: TabSTARData, is_train: bool, batch_size: int = GLOBAL_BATCH) -> DataLoader:
    dataset = DownstreamDataset(data)
    # 0: small in-memory data, no I/O to parallelize; workers here only add overhead and exhaust
    # resources when spun up fresh per dataset.
    return DataLoader(dataset, shuffle=is_train, batch_size=batch_size, collate_fn=pretrain_batch_collator,
                      num_workers=0, pin_memory=True)



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/finetune/epoch_ensemble.py
================================================
from typing import List

import numpy as np


def prediction_spread(val_predictions: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """How much predictions vary across rows: the largest per-class std, or the std of a regression output."""
    per_output_std = np.std(val_predictions.reshape(len(val_predictions), -1), axis=0)
    return float(per_output_std.max())


def select_epochs(val_scores: List[float], spreads: List[float], k: int, collapse_ratio: float) -> List[int]:
    """The k epochs with the lowest val score, skipping collapsed ones: an epoch whose prediction spread fell
    below `collapse_ratio` of the best epoch's predicts near-constant output. Returns 1-based epochs."""
    scores = np.asarray(val_scores)
    spreads = np.asarray(spreads)
    by_score = np.argsort(scores, kind="stable")
    min_spread = spreads[by_score[0]] * collapse_ratio
    healthy = [int(i) + 1 for i in by_score if spreads[i] >= min_spread]
    return healthy[:k]

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/finetune/lora.py

from os.path import basename, exists
from typing import List, Optional

from peft import LoraConfig, get_peft_model, PeftModel
from transformers import AutoTokenizer

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import TEXT_MODEL as E5_SMALL
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.finetune.hyperparams import LORA_ALPHA
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.finetune.lora_params import get_all_lora_modules
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.model import TabSTARModel
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.config import TabStarConfig
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.utils.summary import summarize_model


def load_pretrained_base(model_version: str) -> TabSTARModel:
    """Loads the base model once so a caller finetuning many datasets from the same starting
    checkpoint can reuse it instead of reloading from disk each time. Safe to reuse across LoRA
    finetunes: the base weights stay frozen and unchanged throughout.
    """
    print(f"ðŸ¤© Loading pretrained model version: {model_version}")
    config = TabStarConfig.from_pretrained(model_version)
    model = TabSTARModel.from_pretrained(pretrained_model_name_or_path=model_version, config=config)
    model.tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(E5_SMALL)
    return model


def wrap_with_lora(model: TabSTARModel, lora_r: int, dropout: float, lora_alpha: int = LORA_ALPHA,
                    to_unfreeze: int = 6, is_cls: Optional[bool] = None) -> PeftModel:
    target_modules = get_all_lora_modules(model=model, to_unfreeze=to_unfreeze, is_cls=is_cls)
    lora_config = LoraConfig(
        r=lora_r,
        lora_alpha=lora_r * lora_alpha,
        target_modules=target_modules,
        lora_dropout=dropout,
        bias="none",
    )
    model = get_peft_model(model, lora_config)
    model.print_trainable_parameters()
    # print_all_modules(model=model)
    summarize_model(model=model.base_model.model)
    return model


def load_finetuned(adapter_dirs: List[str], tabstar_version: str) -> PeftModel:
    """One base model carrying every adapter, each named after its directory."""
    for adapter_dir in adapter_dirs:
        if not exists(adapter_dir):
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"Checkpoint path {adapter_dir} does not exist.")
    base_model = TabSTARModel.from_pretrained(tabstar_version, device_map="cpu")
    base_model.tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(E5_SMALL)
    first, *rest = adapter_dirs
    model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(base_model, first, adapter_name=basename(first), device_map='cpu')
    for adapter_dir in rest:
        model.load_adapter(adapter_dir, adapter_name=basename(adapter_dir))
    return model

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/finetune/lora_params.py
================================================
from typing import List, Optional

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.arch.model import TabSTARModel

TO_AVOID = ['bias',
            'LayerNorm',
            'text_encoder.embeddings',
            'output.dense',                 # Text-Encoder
            'intermediate.dense',           # Text-Encoder
            'norm1.weight',
            'norm2.weight',
            'fusion.ln_text',
            'fusion.ln_num',
            'text_weight'
            ]

TO_INCLUDE = [
    # Text Encoder
    'attention.self.query',
    'attention.self.key',
    'attention.self.value',
    'text_encoder.pooler.dense',
    # Numerical Encoder / Fusion
    'numerical_encoder.scalar_embedder',
    'numerical_fusion.scalar_embedder',
    # Interaction Encoder
    'self_attn.in_proj',
    'self_attn.out_proj',
    'linear1',
    'linear2',
    # Prediction
    'cls_head',
    'reg_head',
]

def print_all_modules(model: TabSTARModel):
    print("ðŸ¦œ Printing all model modules, with parameter count:")
    for name, param in model.named_parameters():
        print(f" - {name}: {param.numel()} params")


def get_all_lora_modules(model: TabSTARModel, to_unfreeze: int = 6, is_cls: Optional[bool] = None) -> List[str]:
    """`is_cls=None` wraps both heads (e.g. `assert_lora_coverage`'s static architecture check);
    otherwise only the head the forward pass actually uses for this task is wrapped, since the
    other one gets no gradient either way.
    """
    inactive_head = None
    if is_cls is True:
        inactive_head = 'reg_head'
    elif is_cls is False:
        inactive_head = 'cls_head'
    modules = []
    for name, param in model.named_parameters():
        if is_lora_layer(name=name, to_unfreeze=to_unfreeze):
            if inactive_head is not None and inactive_head in name:
                continue
            if name.endswith('.weight'):
                name = name.replace('.weight', '')
            modules.append(name)
    return modules


def is_lora_layer(name: str, to_unfreeze: int) -> bool:
    assert to_unfreeze > 0, "Must unfreeze at least one text encoder layer"
    layer_to_freeze = list(range(12 - to_unfreeze))
    frozen_to_avoid = [f"text_encoder.encoder.layer.{i}." for i in layer_to_freeze]
    to_avoid = TO_AVOID + frozen_to_avoid
    if any(s in name for s in to_avoid):
        return False
    if any(s in name for s in TO_INCLUDE):
        return True
    raise NotImplementedError(f"Unknown layer type for LoRA: {name}")


def assert_lora_coverage(model: TabSTARModel) -> None:
    """Called once before a pretrain run starts (mirrors `assert_finetunability_installed`):
    a parameter an architecture change adds without registering it in TO_AVOID/TO_INCLUDE makes
    every finetunability check's LoRA-wrapping raise for every dataset, every epoch -- silently,
    since `run_finetunability_check` never propagates that failure, so it would otherwise burn a
    full run with zero finetunability signal before anyone notices. Checked with `to_unfreeze=12`
    (no text-encoder layer frozen): a smaller `to_unfreeze` only avoids more parameters, never
    fewer, so this is the strictest classification any real run could hit.
    """
    uncovered = []
    for name, _ in model.named_parameters():
        try:
            is_lora_layer(name=name, to_unfreeze=12)
        except NotImplementedError:
            uncovered.append(name)
    if uncovered:
        raise NotImplementedError(
            f"{len(uncovered)} parameter(s) not classified in lora_params.py's TO_AVOID/"
            f"TO_INCLUDE: {uncovered}. Register every new layer an architecture change adds "
            f"there before pretraining, or finetunability tracking silently fails every epoch."
        )


'''
 - text_encoder.embeddings.word_embeddings.weight: 11720448 params
 - text_encoder.embeddings.position_embeddings.weight: 196608 params
 - text_encoder.embeddings.token_type_embeddings.weight: 768 params
 - text_encoder.embeddings.LayerNorm.weight: 384 params
 - text_encoder.embeddings.LayerNorm.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.self.query.weight: 147456 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.self.query.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.self.key.weight: 147456 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.self.key.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.self.value.weight: 147456 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.self.value.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.output.dense.weight: 147456 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.output.dense.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.output.LayerNorm.weight: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.attention.output.LayerNorm.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.intermediate.dense.weight: 589824 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.intermediate.dense.bias: 1536 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.output.dense.weight: 589824 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.output.dense.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.output.LayerNorm.weight: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.0.output.LayerNorm.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.1.attention.self.query.weight: 147456 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.1.attention.self.query.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.1.attention.self.key.weight: 147456 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.1.attention.self.key.bias: 384 params
 - text_encoder.encoder.layer.1.attention.self.value.weight: 147456 params

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/finetune/optimizer.py

from torch import nn
from torch.optim import AdamW


def get_lora_optimizer(model: nn.Module, lr: float, wd: float = 0.0) -> AdamW:
    params = [{"params": model.parameters(), "lr": lr, "name": "lora_lr", "weight_decay": wd}]
    optimizer = AdamW(params)
    return optimizer

from typing import List

from pandas import DataFrame


def detect_constant_columns(x: DataFrame) -> List[str]:
    """Columns with nothing to say: one repeated value, or no value at all."""
    return [str(col) for col in x.columns if x[col].nunique() <= 1]


def drop_constant_columns(x: DataFrame, constant_columns: List[str]) -> DataFrame:
    return x.drop(columns=constant_columns, errors="ignore")



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/dates.py
================================================
from typing import Any, Dict
import warnings

import pandas as pd
from pandas import Series, DataFrame
from pandas.core.dtypes.common import is_datetime64_any_dtype
from skrub import DatetimeEncoder


def series_to_dt(s: Series) -> Series:
    s = s.apply(_clean_dirty_date)
    # Catch pandas' silent format-inference warning and re-print it with the column name:
    # otherwise there's no way to tell which column an ambiguous date format came from.
    with warnings.catch_warnings(record=True) as caught:
        warnings.simplefilter("always")
        dt_s = pd.to_datetime(s, errors='coerce')
    for w in caught:
        print(f"⚠️ Date parsing ambiguity in column '{s.name}': {w.message}")
    dt_s = dt_s.apply(_remove_timezone)
    return dt_s


def fit_date_encoders(x: DataFrame) -> Dict[str, DatetimeEncoder]:
    date_encoders = {}
    date_columns = [str(col) for col, dtype in x.dtypes.items() if is_datetime64_any_dtype(dtype)]
    for col in date_columns:
        dt_s = series_to_dt(s=x[col])
        encoder = DatetimeEncoder(add_weekday=True, add_total_seconds=True)
        encoder.fit(dt_s)
        date_encoders[col] = encoder
    return date_encoders


def transform_date_features(x: DataFrame, date_transformers: Dict[str, DatetimeEncoder]) -> DataFrame:
    rows = x.shape[0]
    for col, dt_encoder in date_transformers.items():
        s = series_to_dt(s=x[col])
        dt_df = dt_encoder.transform(s)
        dt_df.index = x.index
        x = x.drop(columns=[col])
        x = pd.concat([x, dt_df], axis=1)
        if x.shape[0] != rows:
            raise ValueError(f"Row mismatch after transforming date column {col}: expected {rows}, got {x.shape}")
    return x


def _remove_timezone(dt):
    if pd.notnull(dt) and getattr(dt, 'tzinfo', None) is not None:
        return dt.tz_localize(None)
    return dt


def _clean_dirty_date(s: Any) -> Any:
    if isinstance(s, str):
        s = s.replace('"', "")
    return s



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/detection.py
================================================
from typing import Any

from pandas import Series

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.preprocessing.nulls import get_valid_values

MAX_NUMERIC_FOR_CATEGORICAL = 50


def is_mostly_numerical(s: Series) -> bool:
    values = get_valid_values(s)
    unique = set(values)
    n_unique = len(unique)
    if n_unique <= MAX_NUMERIC_FOR_CATEGORICAL:
        return False
    non_numerical_unique = [v for v in unique if not is_numeric(v)]
    if len(non_numerical_unique) > 1:
        return False
    return True



def is_numeric(f: Any) -> bool:
    if f is None:
        return False
    if isinstance(f, str):
        return f.isdigit()
    if isinstance(f, (int, float,)):
        return True
    try:
        f = float(f)
        return True
    except ValueError:
        print(f"ValueError: {f} from type {f} cannot be converted to float")
        return False



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/feat_types.py
================================================
from typing import Set, Optional

import pandas as pd
from pandas import DataFrame, Series
from pandas.core.dtypes.common import is_datetime64_any_dtype, is_numeric_dtype, is_object_dtype, is_bool_dtype, is_string_dtype

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.preprocessing.detection import is_mostly_numerical, is_numeric
from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.preprocessing.nulls import convert_numeric_with_missing, MISSING_VALUE, get_valid_values

if int(pd.__version__.split('.')[0]) < 3:
    pd.set_option('future.no_silent_downcasting', True)


def transform_feature_types(x: DataFrame, numerical_features: Set[str]) -> DataFrame:
    new_x = {}
    for col in x.columns:
        if col in numerical_features:
            new_x[col] = convert_series_to_numeric(s=x[col])
        else:
            new_x[col] = convert_series_to_textual(s=x[col])
    new_x = pd.DataFrame(new_x, index=x.index)
    ordered_x = new_x[x.columns]
    return ordered_x


def detect_numerical_features(x: DataFrame) -> Set[str]:
    return {col for col in x.columns if is_numerical_feature(s=x[col])}


def is_numerical_feature(s: Series) -> bool:
    if is_datetime64_any_dtype(s.dtype):
        raise TypeError(f"At this point, dates should have already been transformed.")
    elif len(get_valid_values(s)) == 0:
        return False
    elif is_numeric_dtype(s.dtype) or is_mostly_numerical(s=s):
        return True
    elif is_object_dtype(s.dtype) or is_string_dtype(s.dtype) or is_bool_dtype(s.dtype) or isinstance(s.dtype, pd.CategoricalDtype):
        return False
    else:
        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported dtype {s.dtype} for series {s.name}")


def convert_series_to_numeric(s: Series, missing_value: Optional[str] = None) -> Series:
    if pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(s):
        return s.astype(float)
    non_numeric_indices = [not is_numeric(f) for f in s]
    if not any(non_numeric_indices):
        return s.astype(float)
    if missing_value is None:
        unique_non_numeric = s[non_numeric_indices].unique()
        if len(unique_non_numeric) != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"Missing values detected are {unique_non_numeric}. Should be only one!")
        missing_value = unique_non_numeric[0]
    return convert_numeric_with_missing(s=s, missing_value=missing_value)


def convert_series_to_textual(s: Series):
    return s.astype(object).fillna(MISSING_VALUE).astype(str)

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/nulls.py

from typing import List, Any, Optional, Set

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas import Series

MISSING_VALUE = "Unknown Value"


def raise_if_null_target(y: Series):
    missing = y.isnull()
    y_missing = missing.sum()
    if y_missing > 0:
        raise ValueError(f"Target variable {y.name} has {y_missing} null values, please handle them before training.")


def get_invalid_indices(ls: Series) -> Set[int]:
    return {i for i, x in enumerate(ls) if _get_non_null_value(x) is None}


def get_valid_values(ls: Series) -> List:
    return [x for x in ls if _get_non_null_value(x) is not None]

def _get_non_null_value(x: Any) -> Optional[Any]:
    if isinstance(x, str):
        return x
    if pd.isna(x):
        return None
    if not np.isfinite(x):
        return None
    if pd.isnull(x):
        return None
    return x


def convert_numeric_with_missing(s: pd.Series, missing_value: Any) -> pd.Series:
    return s.apply(lambda x: x if x != missing_value else np.nan).astype(float)



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/numerical.py
================================================
import warnings
from typing import Tuple

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler, MinMaxScaler, QuantileTransformer

# TODO: add / test: tanh_z, RobustScaler, log1p_std, power, sigmoid,
# - power transform (sign(x) * sqrt(abs(x)))
# - log1p standardized: np.log1p(np.clip(safe_values, a_min=0, a_max=None)); self.log1p_scaler.transform(log1p_val)
# - robust: RobustScaler(); self.robust.transform(safe_values)

Z_MAX_ABS = 3
MINMAX_MARGIN = 5

class RichNumericalTransformer:

    def __init__(self, d_num: int):
        self.d_num = d_num
        self.scaler = StandardScaler()
        self.minmax = MinMaxScaler()
        self.quantile = QuantileTransformer(output_distribution='uniform')

    @classmethod
    def fit_series(cls, s: pd.Series, d_num: int) -> "RichNumericalTransformer":
        transformer = RichNumericalTransformer(d_num=d_num)
        transformer.fit(s)
        return transformer

    def fit(self, s: pd.Series):
        values = s.values.reshape(-1, 1)
        valid = ~np.isnan(values).flatten()
        safe_values = values[valid]
        self.scaler.fit(safe_values)
        self.minmax.fit(safe_values)
        with warnings.catch_warnings():
            # UserWarning: n_quantiles (1000) is greater than the total number of samples
            warnings.simplefilter("ignore", category=UserWarning)
            self.quantile.fit(safe_values)
        return self

    def transform(self, s: pd.Series):
        values = s.values.reshape(-1, 1)
        nan_mask = np.isnan(values).flatten()
        # Impute for transform, but keep track of missing for the flag
        impute_mean = np.nanmean(values)
        safe_values = np.where(nan_mask, impute_mean, values.flatten()).reshape(-1, 1)
        z, outlier = self.get_clipped_z_and_outliers(safe_values)
        mm = self.minmax.transform(safe_values)
        mm = np.clip(mm, -MINMAX_MARGIN, 1 + MINMAX_MARGIN)
        quant = self.quantile.transform(safe_values)
        missing = nan_mask.astype(float).reshape(-1, 1)
        # Ones vector, to differentiate from non-numerical features
        ones = np.ones_like(values)
        all_vectors = [z, mm, quant, outlier, missing, ones]
        assert len(all_vectors) >= self.d_num, f"{len(all_vectors)} vs {self.d_num}"
        all_vectors = all_vectors[:self.d_num]
        vec = np.hstack(all_vectors)
        assert vec.shape[1] == self.d_num
        vec = np.nan_to_num(vec, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        return vec

    def get_clipped_z_and_outliers(self, safe_values: np.ndarray) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
        z_raw = self.scaler.transform(safe_values).flatten()
        # Careful: must compute outliers BEFORE clipping
        outlier = (np.abs(z_raw) > Z_MAX_ABS).astype(float).reshape(-1, 1)
        z = np.clip(z_raw, -Z_MAX_ABS, Z_MAX_ABS).reshape(-1, 1)
        return z, outlier


================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/scaler.py
================================================
from pandas import Series
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.preprocessing.nulls import get_invalid_indices

Z_MAX_ABS_VAL = 3


def fit_standard_scaler(s: Series) -> StandardScaler:
    s = s.copy().dropna()
    scaler = StandardScaler()
    scaler.fit(s.values.reshape(-1, 1))
    return scaler

def transform_clipped_z_scores(s: Series, scaler: StandardScaler, allow_null: bool = False) -> Series:
    invalid = get_invalid_indices(s)
    s = s.copy()
    if allow_null:
        s = s.fillna(s.mean())
    s_val = scaler.transform(s.values.reshape(-1, 1)).flatten()
    s_val = s_val.clip(-Z_MAX_ABS_VAL, Z_MAX_ABS_VAL)
    if invalid:
        s_val[sorted(invalid)] = 0
    s = Series(s_val, index=s.index, name=s.name)
    return s

from typing import Tuple, Optional

from pandas import DataFrame, Series, SparseDtype


def densify_objects(x: DataFrame, y: Optional[Series]) -> Tuple[DataFrame, Optional[Series]]:
    for col in x.columns:
        x[col] = densify_series(x[col])
    if y is not None:
        y = densify_series(y)
    return x, y



def densify_series(s: Series) -> Series:
    if not isinstance(s.dtype, SparseDtype):
        return s
    return s.sparse.to_dense()



================================================
FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/splits.py
================================================
import random
from typing import Tuple, Optional

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas import DataFrame, Series
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split

from tabstar.tabstarv1p1.constants import SEED

TEST_RATIO = 0.1
MAX_TEST_SIZE = 2000
VAL_RATIO = 0.1
MAX_VAL_SIZE = 1000

def split_to_test(x: DataFrame, y: Series, is_cls: bool, fold: int = -1, train_examples: Optional[int] = None) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    test_size = int(len(y) * TEST_RATIO)
    test_size = min(test_size, MAX_TEST_SIZE)
    if (train_examples is not None) and len(x) > train_examples:
        test_size = len(x) - train_examples
    x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = do_split(x=x, y=y, test_size=test_size, is_cls=is_cls, fold=fold)
    return x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test

def split_to_val(x: DataFrame, y: Series, is_cls: bool, fold: int = -1, val_ratio: float = VAL_RATIO) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    val_size = int(len(y) * val_ratio)
    val_size = min(val_size, MAX_VAL_SIZE)
    x_train, x_val, y_train, y_val = do_split(x=x, y=y, test_size=val_size, is_cls=is_cls, fold=fold)
    return x_train, x_val, y_train, y_val


def do_split(x: DataFrame, y: Series, test_size: int, is_cls: bool, fold: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    if not x.index.equals(y.index):
        raise ValueError("X and y must share identical indices. Construct y as: y = pd.Series(y, index=X.index)")
    random_state = SEED + fold
    if not is_cls:
        return train_test_split(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state)
    num_classes = y.nunique()
    if num_classes == 2:
        return _split_for_binary_classification(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state)
    has_rare_class = y.value_counts().min() <= 1
    if has_rare_class:
        return _split_with_rare_classes(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state)
    return train_test_split(x, y, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state, stratify=y)


def _split_with_rare_classes(x: DataFrame, y: Series, test_size: int, random_state: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    singleton_classes = y.value_counts()[y.value_counts() == 1].index
    is_singleton = y.isin(singleton_classes)
    x_single = x[is_singleton]
    y_single = y[is_singleton]
    x_rest = x[~is_singleton]
    y_rest = y[~is_singleton]

    rest_classes = len(set(y_rest))
    test_size = max(test_size, rest_classes)
    x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(x_rest, y_rest, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state,
                                                        stratify=y_rest)
    x_train, y_train = merge_splits(x1=x_train, x2=x_single, y1=y_train, y2=y_single, random_state=random_state)
    return x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test


def _split_for_binary_classification(x: DataFrame, y: Series, test_size: int, random_state: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, DataFrame, Series, Series]:
    test_indices = []
    y_values = sorted(set(y))
    assert len(y_values) == 2, f"Expected 2 classes, got {y_values}"
    random_number_generator = np.random.RandomState(random_state)
    for v in y_values:
        v_indices = y[y == v].index.tolist()
        v_idx = random_number_generator.choice(v_indices)
        test_indices.append(v_idx)
    x_single_test = x.loc[test_indices]
    y_single_test = y.loc[test_indices]
    x_rest = x.drop(index=test_indices)
    y_rest = y.drop(index=test_indices)
    x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(x_rest, y_rest, test_size=test_size, random_state=random_state,
                                                        stratify=y_rest)
    x_test, y_test = merge_splits(x1=x_test, x2=x_single_test, y1=y_test, y2=y_single_test, random_state=random_state)
    return x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test



def merge_splits(x1: DataFrame, x2: DataFrame, y1: Series, y2: Series, random_state: int) -> Tuple[DataFrame, Series]:
    x_train = pd.concat([x1, x2])
    y_train = pd.concat([y1, y2])
    shuffled = x_train.sample(frac=1, random_state=random_state).index
    x = x_train.loc[shuffled]
    y = y_train.loc[shuffled]
    return x, y

FILE: src/tabstar/tabstarv1p1/preprocessing/target.py
